```python
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int

        rec, track = [0], defaultdict(int) 
        ct = start = ptr1 = ptr2 = 0

        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)): return 0

        s1 = ''.join(char for char in s1 if char in set(s2))
        
        while True:
            for char in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(char, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(char)
                start = ptr+1
            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1: return (len(rec)-2)//n2

            if ptr not in track: track[ptr] = len(0rec)-1
            
            else: break
        
        cycleStart = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle1, cycle2 = ct+1 - cycleStart, len(rec)-1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycleStart
        
        rem = cycleStart + self.calculateRemainder(rest, cycle1)

        while rec[ptr2] <= rem: ptr2+= 1

        return (cycle2 * (rest//cycle1) + ptr2-2)//n2
```

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int

        rec, track = [0], defaultdict(int) 
        ct = start = ptr1 = ptr2 = 0

        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)): return 0

        s1 = ''.join(char for char in s1 if char in set(s2))
        
        while True:
            for char in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(char, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(char)
                start = ptr+1
            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1: return (len(rec)-2)//n2

            if ptr not in track: track[ptr] = len(0rec)-1
            
            else: break
        
        cycleStart = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle1, cycle2 = ct+1 - cycleStart, len(rec)-1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycleStart
        
        rem = cycleStart + self.calculateRemainder(rest, cycle1)

        while rec[ptr2] <= rem: ptr2+= 1

        return (cycle2 * (rest//cycle1) + ptr2-2)//n2
```

We need compute max m such that (s2*n2)^m is subsequence of s1^n1. Equivalent: count how many times s2 can be matched as subsequence in s1^n1; let cnt = max repetitions of s2 in s1^n1. Conversely if (s2*n2)^m is subsequence, then (s2)^(n2*m) is subsequence, so cnt >= n2*m. Thus max m = floor(cnt/n2). So need count max number of complete s2 subsequences in s1 repeated n1 times.

Classic solution: simulate matching s2 in repeated s1, record state after each s2 match: position in s1 within current s1 block? Let's recall known solution:
```
def getMaxRepetitions(s1, n1, s2, n2):
    if not set(s2) <= set(s1): return 0
    repeatCount = [0]*(len(s2)+1)
    nextIndex = [0]*(len(s2)+1)
    j = 0
    cnt = 0
    for k in range(1, n1+1):
        for i in range(len(s1)):
            if s1[i] == s2[j]:
                j += 1
                if j == len(s2):
                    j = 0
                    cnt += 1
        repeatCount[k] = cnt
        nextIndex[k] = j
        for p in range(k):
            if nextIndex[p] == j:
                prevCnt = repeatCount[p]
                intervalCnt = cnt - prevCnt
                intervalLen = k - p
                remaining = n1 - p
                cnt = prevCnt + (remaining // intervalLen) * intervalCnt
                for q in range(remaining % intervalLen):
                    for i in range(len(s1)):
                        if s1[i] == s2[j]:
                            j += 1
                            if j == len(s2):
                                j = 0
                                cnt += 1
                break
        else:
            continue
        break
    return cnt // n2
```
This is standard. Need maybe optimize. Could implement simpler.

Approach 1: Simulate through n1 copies of s1, count s2 matches, detect cycle in state (position in s2 after each s1 block). Since n1 can be large, cycle detection.

Let's define:
- `cnt` = number of complete s2 matches so far.
- `idx` = index in s2 of next character to match (0..len(s2)-1).
- For each s1 block (k from 1 to n1):
  - Scan characters of s1:
    - if char == s2[idx]:
      - idx += 1
      - if idx == len(s2): cnt += 1; idx = 0
  - After block, record `cnt` and `idx`.
  - If we have seen this `idx` at an earlier block `prev_k`, then cycle found:
    - `prev_cnt` = count at prev_k
    - `cycle_cnt` = cnt - prev_cnt (number of s2 matches per cycle)
    - `cycle_len` = k - prev_k (number of s1 blocks per cycle)
    - remaining blocks after prev_k: `n1 - prev_k`
    - full cycles = remaining // cycle_len
    - cnt = prev_cnt + full_cycles * cycle_cnt
    - Then need process remaining blocks = remaining % cycle_len manually from current idx? Wait if we break at k, current state after k blocks. We can compute total count for n1 by using prev_k as start. Let's derive.

Suppose after processing k blocks, state idx equals state after prev_k. Let `cnt_k` count after k blocks, `cnt_prev` after prev_k. The segment from prev_k+1 to k has cycle_len blocks and produced cycle_cnt matches, ending at same idx. For n1 blocks, if n1 >= k, we can use:
```
remaining = n1 - prev_k
cycles = remaining // cycle_len
cnt = cnt_prev + cycles * cycle_cnt
idx = idx_at_prev (same as current)
rest = remaining % cycle_len
for _ in range(rest):
    process one s1 block from current idx
```
But if we break at k, we have already processed k blocks, not just prev_k + rest. We can set `cnt = cnt_prev + cycles * cycle_cnt`, then process `rest` blocks. Need ensure `rest` < cycle_len. We don't need process already processed blocks beyond rest. This works because cycle_len is period in terms of blocks and idx repeats. But note `cnt_prev` is count after prev_k blocks, and `idx` after prev_k blocks. We can reset idx to idx_prev (same). Then process `rest` blocks. We don't need include the k blocks processed after prev_k except as part of cycles. Since `remaining // cycle_len` includes them. Good.

But in standard loop, if cycle found at k, we can compute and break. Need be careful if n1 <= k? If n1 <= k, we already processed enough, just return cnt//n2. But if n1 can be less than k, no cycle needed. We can just simulate until n1. If n1 large and cycle found at k <= n1, compute. If cycle found at k > n1? impossible if we stop at n1. We can check after each block if k == n1 return. But if cycle found before n1, compute. Need not process all n1.

Simpler:
```
seen = {}  # idx -> (block_index, count)
cnt = 0
idx = 0
k = 0
while k < n1:
    # process one s1 block
    for ch in s1:
        if ch == s2[idx]:
            idx += 1
            if idx == len(s2):
                cnt += 1
                idx = 0
    k += 1
    if idx in seen:
        prev_k, prev_cnt = seen[idx]
        cycle_len = k - prev_k
        cycle_cnt = cnt - prev_cnt
        remaining = n1 - k
        cnt += (remaining // cycle_len) * cycle_cnt
        rest = remaining % cycle_len
        # process rest blocks from current idx
        for _ in range(rest):
            for ch in s1:
                if ch == s2[idx]:
                    idx += 1
                    if idx == len(s2):
                        cnt += 1
                        idx = 0
        break
    seen[idx] = (k, cnt)
return cnt // n2
```

Let's define correct function:

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in set(s2))
        
        cnt = 0
        idx = 0
        seen = {0: (0, 0)}
        k = 0
        while k < n1:
            for ch in s1:
                if ch == s2[idx]:
                    idx += 1
                    if idx == len(s2):
                        cnt += 1
                        idx = 0
            k += 1
            
            if idx in seen:
                prev_k, prev_cnt = seen[idx]
                cycle_len = k - prev_k
                cycle_cnt = cnt - prev_cnt
                remaining = n1 - k
                cnt += (remaining // cycle_len) * cycle_cnt
                rest = remaining % cycle_len
                for _ in range(rest):
                    for ch in s1:
                        if ch == s2[idx]:
                            idx += 1
                            if idx == len(s2):
                                cnt += 1
                                idx = 0
                break
            seen[idx] = (k, cnt)
        return cnt // n2
```

Now, let's compare with known standard solution. Standard solution often records `nextIndex` and `count` after each s1 block, and when repeat, computes:
```
if k == n1: return count[n1] // n2
for start in range(k):
    if nextIndex[start] == nextIndex[k]:
        prevCount = count[start]
        patternCount = (count[k] - prevCount) * ((n1 - start) // (k - start))
        remainCount = count[start + (n1 - start) % (k - start)] - prevCount
        return (prevCount + patternCount + remainCount) // n2
```

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        allowed = set(s2)
        if not allowed.issubset(s1):
            return 0
        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in allowed)

        cnt = 0
        idx = 0
        seen = {0: (0, 0)}
        k = 0

        while k < n1:
            for ch in s1:
                if ch == s2[idx]:
                    idx += 1
                    if idx == len(s2):
                        cnt += 1
                        idx = 0
            k += 1

            if idx in seen:
                prev_k, prev_cnt = seen[idx]
                cycle_len = k - prev_k
                cycle_cnt = cnt - prev_cnt

                remaining = n1 - k
                cnt += (remaining // cycle_len) * cycle_cnt

                for _ in range(remaining % cycle_len):
                    for ch in s1:
                        if ch == s2[idx]:
                            idx += 1
                            if idx == len(s2):
                                cnt += 1
                                idx = 0
                break

            seen[idx] = (k, cnt)

        return cnt // n2
```

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        repeat = [0] * (len(s2) + 1)
        next_idx = [0] * (len(s2) + 1)
        j, cnt = 0, 0
        for k in range(1, n1 + 1):
            for i in range(len(s1)):
                if s1[i] == s2[j]:
                    j += 1
                    if j == len(s2):
                        j = 0
                        cnt += 1
            repeat[k] = cnt
            next_idx[k] = j
            for start in range(k):
                if next_idx[start] == j:
                    prev_cnt = repeat[start]
                    pattern_cnt = (cnt - prev_cnt) * ((n1 - start) // (k - start))
                    remain_cnt = repeat[start + (n1 - start) % (k - start)] - prev_cnt
                    return (prev_cnt + pattern_cnt + remain_cnt) // n2
        return cnt // n2
```
This standard solution stores `repeat` for every k and uses `next_idx[0]`? It initializes arrays with 0, so start=0 represents initial state. It loops k from 1 to n1. If n1 small, returns cnt. Our algorithm equivalent but doesn't store all counts; it processes rest manually. Good.

```
rec, track = [0], defaultdict(int) 
ct = start = ptr1 = ptr2 = 0

if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)): return 0

s1 = ''.join(char for char in s1 if char in set(s2))

while True:
    for char in s2:
        ptr = s1.find(char, start)
        if ptr == -1:
            ct += 1
            ptr = s1.find(char)
        start = ptr+1
    rec.append(ct + 1)

    if rec[-1] > n1: return (len(rec)-2)//n2

    if ptr not in track: track[ptr] = len(0rec)-1
    else: break

cycleStart = rec[track[ptr]]
cycle1, cycle2 = ct+1 - cycleStart, len(rec)-1 - track[ptr]
rest = n1 - cycleStart

rem = cycleStart + self.calculateRemainder(rest, cycle1)

while rec[ptr2] <= rem: ptr2+= 1

return (cycle2 * (rest//cycle1) + ptr2-2)//n2
```

Now, let's maybe compare our algorithm to a known accepted Python solution to ensure no subtle bug with cycle detection using `idx` only. Known solution:
```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        repeat = [0] * (len(s2) + 1)
        next_idx = [0] * (len(s2) + 1)
        j, cnt = 0, 0
        for k in range(1, n1 + 1):
            for i in range(len(s1)):
                if s1[i] == s2[j]:
                    j += 1
                    if j == len(s2):
                        j = 0
                        cnt += 1
            repeat[k] = cnt
            next_idx[k] = j
            for start in range(k):
                if next_idx[start] == j:
                    prev_cnt = repeat[start]
                    pattern_cnt = (cnt - prev_cnt) * ((n1 - start) // (k - start))
                    remain_cnt = repeat[start + (n1 - start) % (k - start)] - prev_cnt
                    return (prev_cnt + pattern_cnt + remain_cnt) // n2
        return cnt // n2
```
This standard solution initializes `repeat` and `next_idx` with zeros, so `start=0` represents initial state. It loops k from 1 to n1. If cycle found, it returns immediately using precomputed `repeat` for remainder. Our code processes remainder manually. Both should match. Let's test our code against this standard for a case where cycle found at k=2, prev_k=1, n1=5, cycle_len=1. We did.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
>
```python

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        allowed = set(s2)
        if not allowed.issubset(s1):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in allowed)

        cnt = 0
        idx = 0
        seen = {0: (0, 0)}
        k = 0

        while k < n1:
            for ch in s1:
                if ch == s2[idx]:
                    idx += 1
                    if idx == len(s2):
                        cnt += 1
                        idx = 0
            k += 1

            if idx in seen:
                prev_k, prev_cnt = seen[idx]
                cycle_len = k - prev_k
                cycle_cnt = cnt - prev_cnt

                remaining = n1 - k
                cnt += (remaining // cycle_len) * cycle_cnt

                for _ in range(remaining % cycle_len):
                    for ch in s1:
                        if ch == s2[idx]:
                            idx += 1
                            if idx == len(s2):
                                cnt += 1
                                idx = 0
                break

            seen[idx] = (k, cnt)

        return cnt // n2

```

=== FIXED CODE ===
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        allowed = set(s2)
        if not allowed.issubset(s1):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in allowed)

        cnt = 0
        idx = 0
        seen = {0: (0, 0)}
        k = 0

        while k < n1:
            for ch in s1:
                if ch == s2[idx]:
                    idx += 1
                    if idx == len(s2):
                        cnt += 1
                        idx = 0
            k += 1

            if idx in seen:
                prev_k, prev_cnt = seen[idx]
                cycle_len = k - prev_k
                cycle_cnt = cnt - prev_cnt

                remaining = n1 - k
                cnt += (remaining // cycle_len) * cycle_cnt

                for _ in range(remaining % cycle_len):
                    for ch in s1:
                        if ch == s2[idx]:
                            idx += 1
                            if idx == len(s2):
                                cnt += 1
                                idx = 0
                break

            seen[idx] = (k, cnt)

        return cnt // n2
